\mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \left\{r \in [ m ] \bigg | \exists \ell \in [ 2 ] \colon \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(0)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \Lambda_ {i, \ell} ^ {(0)} \Big (1 - O \big (\frac {1}{\log k} \big) \Big) \right\}
$$

Intuition. If a neuron  $r \in [m]$  is not in  $\mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$ , it means that for both  $\ell \in \{1,2\}$ , the correlation  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(0)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle$  at the random initialization is, by a non-trivial factor, smaller than  $\Lambda_{i,\ell}^{(0)}$  — the largest correlation between  $\langle w_{i,r'}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle$  among all neurons. In words, this means the magnitude of  $v_{i,1}$  and  $v_{i,2}$  inside the random initialization  $w_{i,r}^{(0)}$  is non-trivially lagging behind, comparing to other neurons. We shall prove that, through the course of the training, those neurons  $r$  will lose the lottery and not learn anything useful for the output label  $i \in [k]$ . (This corresponds to Induction Hypothesis C.3i later.)

Fact C.1. With probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^5 k)}$ , we have  $|\mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}| \leq m_0 \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} O(\log^5 k)$ .

(The proof of Fact C.1 follows from standard analysis on Gaussian variables, see Proposition H.1.) Suppose we denote by  $S_{i,\ell} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbb{E}_{(X,y) \sim \mathcal{Z}_m}\left[\mathbb{1}_{y=i} \sum_{p \in P_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)} z_p^q\right]$ . Then, define

$$
\mathcal {M} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \left\{(i, \ell^ {*}) \in [ k ] \times [ 2 ] \Big | \Lambda_ {i, \ell^ {*}} ^ {(0)} \geq \Lambda_ {i, 3 - \ell^ {*}} ^ {(0)} \left(\frac {S _ {i , 3 - \ell^ {*}}}{S _ {i , \ell^ {*}}}\right) ^ {\frac {1}{q - 2}} \left(1 + \frac {1}{\log^ {2} (m)}\right) \right\} \tag {C.2}
$$

Intuition. If  $(i,\ell)\in \mathcal{M}$ , we shall prove that the feature  $v_{i,\ell}$  has a higher chance than  $v_{i,3 - \ell}$  to be learned by the model. (This is because, after an appropriate scaling factor defined by the training data,  $v_{i,\ell}$  correlates more with the network's random initialization comparing to  $v_{i,3 - \ell}$ .)

Our next proposition states that, for every  $i \in [k]$ , with decent probability at least one of  $(i,1)$  or  $(i,2)$  shall be in  $\mathcal{M}$ . But more importantly, our later Induction Hypothesis C.3e ensures that, during the entire training process, if  $(i,3 - \ell) \in \mathcal{M}$ , then  $v_{i,\ell}$  must be missing from the learner network. They together imply that test accuracy on single-view data cannot exceed 49.99%, as one of the views shall be missing.

On the other hand, our next proposition also ensures that the "weaker" feature among the two, still has some non-negligible chance to be picked up by the random initialization. This is behind the reason that why ensemble works in our later proofs.

Proposition C.2. Suppose  $m \leq \mathrm{poly}(k)$ . We have the following properties about  $\mathcal{M}$ .

- For every  $i \in [k]$ , at most one of  $(i, 1)$  or  $(i, 2)$  is in  $\mathcal{M}$  (obvious).  
- For every  $i \in [k]$ , suppose  $S_{i,\ell} \geq S_{i,3 - \ell}$ , then  $\mathbf{Pr}\left[(i,3 - \ell) \in \mathcal{M}\right] \geq m^{-O(1)}$ .  
- For every  $i \in [k]$ ,  $\mathbf{Pr}\left[(i,1) \in \mathcal{M} \text{ or } (i,3) \in \mathcal{M}\right] \geq 1 - o(1)$ .

(Proposition C.2 is a result of the anti-concentration of the maximum of Gaussian, see Appendix H.)

We are now ready to state our induction hypothesis.

Induction Hypothesis C.3. For every  $\ell \in [2]$ , for every  $r \in [m]$ , for every  $(X,y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$  and  $i \in [k]$ , or for every  $(X,y) \in \mathcal{Z}_s$  and  $i \in [k] \setminus \{y\}$ :

(a) For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)$ , we have:  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle = \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle z_p \pm \widetilde{o}(\sigma_0)$ .  
(b) For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}(X) \setminus \left(\mathcal{P}_{v_{i,1}}(X) \cup \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,2}}(X)\right)$ , we have:  $|\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle| \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$ .  
(c) For every  $p\in [P]\setminus \mathcal{P}(X)$ , we have:  $|\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_p\rangle |\leq \widetilde{O} (\sigma_0\gamma k)$ .

In addition, for every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$ , every  $i\in [k]$ , every  $r\in [m]$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$

(d) For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)$ , we have:  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle = \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle z_p + \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, \xi_p \rangle \pm \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0 \gamma k)$  
(e) For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)$ , if  $(i,3 - \ell) \in \mathcal{M}$  we have:  $|\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_p\rangle |\leq \widetilde{O} (\sigma_0)$ .  
(f) For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)$ , if  $r \in [m] \setminus \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$  we have:  $|\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle| \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$ .

Moreover, we have for every  $i\in [k]$

(g)  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\geq \Omega (\sigma_0)$  and  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\leq \widetilde{O} (1)$  
(h) for every  $r \in [m]$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , it holds that  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle \geq -\widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$ .  
(i) for every  $r \in [m] \setminus \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , it holds that  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$ .

Intuition. The first three items in Induction Hypothesis C.3 essentially say that, when studying the correlation between  $w_{i,r}$  with a multi-view data, or between  $w_{i,r}$  with a single-view data (but  $y \neq i$ ), the correlation is about  $\langle w_{i,r}, v_{i,1} \rangle$  and  $\langle w_{i,r}, v_{i,2} \rangle$  and the remaining terms are sufficiently small. (Of course, this requires a careful proof.) We shall later prove that at least one of  $\Lambda_{i,1}^{(t)}$  or  $\Lambda_{i,2}^{(t)}$  is large after training. Therefore, using the first three items, we can argue that all multi-view data are classified correctly.

The middle three items in Induction Hypothesis C.3 essentially say that, when studying the correlation between  $w_{i,r}$  with a single-view data  $(X,y)$  with  $y = i$ , then the correlation also has a significant noise term  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},\xi_p\rangle$ . This term shall become useful for us to argue that single-view data can be all memorized (through for instance memorizing the noise).

The remaining items in Induction Hypothesis C.3 are just some regularization statements.

# D Single Model: Technical Proofs

We devote this section to prove that Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for every iteration  $t \leq T$ , and in the next Section E, we state how the induction hypothesis easily implies our main theorems for single model and ensemble model.

Parameter D.1. We state the range of parameters for our proofs in this section to hold.

$\varrho = \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  
$\Gamma = \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  
$q\geq 3$  and  $\sigma_0^{q - 2} = \frac{1}{k}$  
-  $N_{s} \leq \widetilde{o}(k / \rho)$  and  $N_{s} \leq \frac{k^{2}}{s} \rho^{q - 1}$ .  
$\gamma \leq \widetilde{O}\left(\frac{\sigma_0}{k}\right)$  and  $\gamma^q \leq \widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{1}{k^{q-1}mP}\right)$  
polylog  $(k)\leq s\leq k^{0.2}$  
$\rho^{q - 1}\geq \frac{1}{k}$

(recall  $\varrho$  is the threshold for the smoothed ReLU activation)

(recall  $\Gamma$  controls off-target feature magnitude in Def. 3.1)

(recall  $w_{i,r}^{(0)}\sim \mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_0^2 I)$  gives the initialization magnitude)

(recall  $N_{s}$  is the size of single-view training data)

(recall  $\gamma$  controls feature noise in Def. 3.1)

(recall  $s$  controls feature sparsity in Def. 3.1)

(recall  $\rho$  controls on-target feature magnitude of single-view data in Def. 3.1)

-  $N \geq N_s \cdot \mathsf{poly}(k)$ ,  $\eta T \geq N \cdot \mathsf{poly}(k)$ , and  $\sqrt{d} \geq \eta T \cdot \mathsf{poly}(k)$ .  
polylog  $(k)\leq m\leq \widetilde{O} (\frac{1}{s\sigma_0^q})$

Example. A reasonable set of parameters is, up to polylogarithmic factors:

$$
q = 4, \quad \sigma_ {0} = \frac {1}{\sqrt {k}}, \quad \rho = \frac {1}{k ^ {0 . 2}}, \quad m \le k, \quad s \le k ^ {0. 2}, \quad N _ {s} \le k ^ {1. 2}, \quad P \le k ^ {2}, \quad \gamma \le \frac {1}{k ^ {1 . 5}}.
$$

Theorem D.2. Under Parameter  $D.1$ , for any  $m \in [\widetilde{\Omega}(1), \widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{s\sigma_0^q})]$  and sufficiently small  $\eta \leq \frac{1}{\mathrm{poly}(k)}$ , our Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for all iterations  $t = 0, 1, \ldots, T$ .

# D.1 Gradient Calculations and Function Approximation

Gradient calculations. Recall  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F,X)\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\frac{e^{F_i(X)}}{\sum_{j\in[k]}e^{F_j(X)}}$  . Recall also

Fact D.3. Given data point  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{D}$ , for every  $i\in [k]$ ,  $r\in [m]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F; X, y) = \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} (F, X)\right) \sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\left\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \right\rangle\right) x _ {p} \quad \text {w h e n} i = y (D.1) \\ - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F; X, y) = - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} (F, X) \sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) x _ {p} \quad \text {w h e n} i \neq y (D.2) \\ \end{array}
$$

Now, we also have the following observations:

Claim D.4. If Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$ , and if  $s \leq \widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_0^q m}\right)$  and  $\gamma \leq \widetilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_0 k(mP)^{1/q}}\right)$ , then

for every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}$  and every  $i\in [k]$ :  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X) = O\left(\frac{e^{O(\Lambda_i^{(t)})m_0}}{e^{O(\Lambda_i^{(t)})m_0} + k}\right)$  
for every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$  and  $i\in [k]\setminus \{y\}$  ..  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X) = O\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_y(F^{(t)},X)\right)$

Proof of Claim D.4. Recall  $F_{i}^{(t)}(X) = \sum_{r\in [m]}\sum_{p\in [P]}\widetilde{\mathsf{ReLU}} (\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_{p}\rangle)$ . For every  $r\notin \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$ , using Induction Hypothesis C.3i we have

$$
\sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} \left(\left\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p} \right\rangle\right) \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\sigma_ {0} ^ {q}\right) \cdot s + \widetilde {O} \left(\left(\sigma_ {0} \gamma k\right) ^ {q}\right) \cdot P \leq \frac {1}{m p o l y l o g (k)}
$$

so they sum up to at most  $\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ . For any  $r \in \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} (\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p} \rangle) \leq \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \left[ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right] ^ {+} \cdot \left(\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i}, \ell} (X)} z _ {p}\right) + \widetilde {o} (\sigma_ {0}) + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0} ^ {q}) \cdot s + \widetilde {O} ((\sigma_ {0} \gamma k) ^ {q}) \cdot P \\ \leq \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \left[ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right] ^ {+} \cdot \left(\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i}, \ell} (X)} z _ {p}\right) + O \left(\frac {1}{m _ {0}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall from Def. 3.1 we have  $\sum_{p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_i,\ell}(X)}z_p\leq O(1)$ ; and furthermore when  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$  and  $i\in [k]\setminus \{y\}$  we have  $\sum_{p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_i,\ell}(X)}z_p\leq \Gamma = \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ . In the former case, we have

$$
0 \leq F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) \leq m _ {0} \cdot \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)} \cdot O (1) + O (1)
$$

and this proves the first bound; in the latter case we have

$$
0 \leq F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) \leq m _ {0} \cdot \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)} \cdot \Gamma + O (1) \leq O (1) \tag {D.3}
$$

and this proves the second bound.

Definition D.5. For each data point  $X$ , we consider a value  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X)$  given as:

$$
V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)} \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) z _ {p}
$$

Definition D.6. We define four error terms that shall be used frequently in our proofs.

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {1} := \widetilde {O} \left(\sigma_ {0} ^ {q - 1}\right) \gamma s \quad \mathcal {E} _ {2, i, r} (X) := O \left(\gamma \left(V _ {i, r, 1} (X) + V _ {i, r, 2} (X)\right)\right)
$$

$$
\mathcal {E} _ {3} := \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0} \gamma k) ^ {q - 1} \gamma P \quad \mathcal {E} _ {4, j, \ell} (X) := \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)}
$$

we first bound the positive gradient (namely for  $i = y$ ):

Claim D.7 (positive gradient). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$ . For every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}$ , every  $r \in [m]$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , and  $i = y$ , we have

(a)  $\left\langle -\nabla_{w_{i,r}}L\left(F^{(t)};X,y\right),v_{i,\ell}\right\rangle \geq \left(V_{i,r,\ell}(X) - \widetilde{O} (\sigma_pP)\right)\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X)\right)$  
(b)  $\left\langle -\nabla_{w_{i,r}}L\left(F^{(t)};X,y\right),v_{i,\ell}\right\rangle \leq \left(V_{i,r,\ell}(X) + \mathcal{E}_1 + \mathcal{E}_3\right)\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X)\right)$  
(c) For every  $j \in [k] \setminus \{i\}$ ,

$$
\left| \left\langle \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F ^ {(t)}, X, y), v _ {j, \ell} \right\rangle \right| \leq \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \left(\mathcal {E} _ {2, i, r} (X) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathcal {E} _ {4, j, \ell} (X)\right)
$$

Proof of Claim D.7. We drop the superscript  ${}^{(t)}$  for notational simplicity. Using the gradient formula from (D.1) (in the case of  $i = y$ ), and the orthogonality among feature vectors, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\langle - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F; X, y), v _ {j, \ell} \right\rangle = (1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} (F, X)) \times \\ \left(\mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)} \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {j, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathrm {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) z _ {p} + \sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\mathrm {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) \alpha_ {p, v _ {j, \ell}} \pm \sum_ {p \in [ P ]} | \langle v _ {j, \ell}, \xi_ {p} \rangle |\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Using the randomness of  $\xi_{p}$ , we have (recalling  $\sigma_{p} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  and  $\gamma \leq \frac{1}{k}$ )

$$
\sum_ {p \in [ P ]} | \langle v _ {j, \ell}, \xi_ {p} \rangle | \leq \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} \cdot s + \frac {\gamma k}{\sqrt {d}} \cdot P) \ll \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} \cdot P)
$$

When  $j = i$  we have  $v_{i,\ell} \in \mathcal{V}(X)$  so this proves Claim D.7a. Using Induction Hypothesis C.3, we have

- For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,1}}(X) \cup \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,2}}(X)$ , we have:  $\widetilde{\mathrm{ReLU}}'(\langle w_{i,r}, x_p \rangle) \in [0, 1]$ .  
- For every  $p \in \mathcal{P}(X) \setminus \left(\mathcal{P}_{v_{i,1}}(X) \cup \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,2}}(X)\right)$ , we have:  $\widetilde{\mathrm{ReLU}}'(\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle) \in [0, \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0^{q-1})]$ .  
- For every  $p \in [P] \setminus \mathcal{P}(X)$ , we have:  $\widetilde{\mathrm{ReLU}}'(\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle) \in [0, \widetilde{O}((\sigma_0 \gamma k)^{q-1})]$ .

Using the sparsity from Def. 3.1, we have  $|\mathcal{P}(X) \setminus (\mathcal{P}_{v_{i,1}}(X) \cup \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,2}}(X))| \leq \widetilde{O}(s)$ . Combining this with  $\alpha_{p,v} \leq \gamma$ , and setting  $j = i$ , this proves Claim D.7b.

Finally, when  $j \neq i$ , using Induction Hypothesis C.3 we additionally have

- When  $v_{j,\ell} \in \mathcal{V}(X)$  and  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{j,\ell}}(X)$ , we have  $\widetilde{\mathrm{ReLU}}'(\langle w_{i,r}, x_p \rangle) \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0^{q - 1})$ .

- For  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,1}}(X) \cup \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,2}}(X)$ , we have a more precise bound using Induction Hypothesis C.3a:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) \leq \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \widetilde {o} (\sigma_ {0})) \\ \leq \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathrm {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \widetilde {o} (\sigma_ {0})) z _ {p} \leq \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathrm {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) z _ {p} + \widetilde {o} (\sigma_ {0}) = V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) \\ \end{array}
$$

Putting them together proves Claim D.7c.

![](images/8acc96491ebe133565b340c7c2b4c60919f284efdda0d6582321c2abdac939bb.jpg)

We also have the following claim about the negative gradient (namely for  $i \neq y$ ), whose proof is completely symmetric to that of Claim D.7 so we ignore here.

Claim D.8 (negative gradient). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$ . For every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}$ , every  $r \in [m]$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , and  $i \in [k] \setminus \{y\}$ , we have

(a)  $\left\langle -\nabla_{w_{i,r}}L\left(F^{(t)},X,y\right),v_{i,\ell}\right\rangle \geq -\mathbf{logit}_i\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\left(\mathcal{E}_1 + \mathcal{E}_3 + \mathbb{1}_{v_{i,\ell}\in \mathcal{P}(X)}V_{i,r,\ell}(X)\right)$  
(b) For every  $j \in [k]$ :  $\left\langle -\nabla_{w_{i,r}}L\left(F^{(t)},X,y\right),v_{j,\ell}\right\rangle \leq \mathbf{logit}_i\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\widetilde{O} (\sigma_pP)$  
(c) For every  $j\in [k]\setminus \{i\}$ :  $\left\langle -\nabla_{w_{i,r}}L\left(F^{(t)},X,y\right),v_{j,\ell}\right\rangle \geq -\mathbf{logit}_i\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\left(\mathcal{E}_1 + \mathcal{E}_3 + \mathcal{E}_{4,j,\ell}(X)\right)$

Function approximations. Let us denote

$$
\Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {r \in [ m ]} [ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle ] ^ {+} \quad \text {a n d} \quad \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \tag {D.4}
$$

One can easily derive that

Claim D.9 (function approximation). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$  and supposes  $\leq \widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sigma_0^q m})$  and  $\gamma \leq \widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sigma_0 k(mP)^{1/q}})$ . Let  $Z_{i,\ell}^{(t)}(X) \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbb{1}_{v_{i,\ell} \in \mathcal{V}(X)}\left(\sum_{p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}(X)}} z_p\right)$ , we have:

- for every  $t$ , every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$  and  $i \in [k]$ , or for every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_s$  and  $i \in [k] \setminus \{y\}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) = \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \left(\Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \times Z _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} (X)\right) \pm \widetilde {O} \left(\sigma_ {0} + \sigma_ {0} ^ {q} s m + (\sigma_ {0} \gamma k) ^ {q} \cdot P m\right) \\ = \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \left(\Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \times Z _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} (X)\right) \pm O \left(\frac {1}{\text {p o l y l o g} (k)}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

- for every  $(X, y) \sim \mathcal{D}$ , with probability at least  $1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 k)}$  it satisfies for every  $i \in [k]$ ,

$$
F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) = \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \left(\Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \times Z _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} (X)\right) \pm O (\frac {1}{\mathrm {p o l y l o g} (k)})
$$

# D.2 Useful Claims as Consequences of the Induction Hypothesis

In this sub-section we state some consequences of our Induction Hypothesis C.3. They shall be useful in our later proof of the induction hypothesis.

# D.2.1 Correlation Growth

Claim D.10 (growth). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$ , then for every  $i \in [k]$ , suppose  $\Lambda_i^{(t)} \leq O(1 / m_0)$ , then it satisfies

$$
\Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} = \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)} + \Theta \left(\frac {\eta}{k}\right) \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)})
$$

Proof of Claim D.10. Recall  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\max_{r\in [m],\ell \in [2]}\left[\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \right]^+$ .

Now, let us take any  $r \in [m]$  and  $\ell \in [2]$  so that  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde{\Omega}(\sigma_0)$ . We first show a lower bound on the increment. By Claim D.7 and Claim D.8,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) - \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P)\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right. \\ \left. - \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} V _ {i, r, \ell} (X)\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \tag {D.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) = \sum_{p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)}\widetilde{\mathsf{ReLU}}^{\prime}(\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_p\rangle)z_p$ . Using Induction Hypothesis C.3, we know that as long as  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_m$ , or when  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$  but  $i\neq y$ , it satisfies

$$
V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) = \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle z _ {p} \pm \widetilde {o} (\sigma_ {0})\right) z _ {p}
$$

- When  $i = y$  is the correct label, at least when  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_m$ , we have  $\sum_{p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)}z_p\geq 1$ , and together with  $|\mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}|\leq C_p = O(1)$ , this tells us  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X)\geq \Omega (1)\cdot \widetilde{\mathsf{ReLU}'}\Big(\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \Big)$ .  
- When  $i \neq y$  is the wrong label and when  $v_{i,\ell} \in \mathcal{P}(X)$ , we can use  $z_p \leq O(1)$  to derive that  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) \leq O(1) \cdot \widetilde{\mathrm{ReLU}}'(\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle)$ .

Together with  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X)\leq O(\frac{1}{k})$  from Claim D.4, we can derive that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \cdot \Omega (1) - O (1) \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} \frac {1}{k} \right] \cdot \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle\right) \\ - \eta \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\sigma_ {p} P + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3}}{k}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Finally, recall the property of our distribution  $\mathbf{Pr}_{(X,y)\sim \mathcal{D}}\left[v_{i,\ell}\in \mathcal{P}(X)\mid i\neq y\right] = \frac{s}{k}\ll o(1)$ , we derive that

$$
\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \Omega \left(\frac {\eta}{k}\right) \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle\right)
$$

As for the lower bound, using Claim D.7 and Claim D.8 again, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \leq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {\substack {(X, y) \sim \mathcal{Z}}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3}\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right. \\ \left. - \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P)\right) \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

so a completely symmetric argument also shows

$$
\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \leq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + O \left(\frac {\eta}{k}\right) \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle\right)
$$

Claim D.10 immediately gives the following corollary (using  $\Omega(\sigma_0) \leq \Lambda_i^{(0)} \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$ ):

Claim D.11. Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for every iteration. Define thresholds (noticing  $\Lambda_{\emptyset}^{-} \leq \Lambda_{\emptyset}^{+}$ ):

$$
\Lambda_ {\varnothing} ^ {-} \stackrel {{\mathrm {d e f}}} {{=}} \Theta \left(\frac {\varrho}{\log k}\right) = \widetilde {\Theta} (1) \quad a n d \quad \Lambda_ {\varnothing} ^ {+} \stackrel {{\mathrm {d e f}}} {{=}} \Theta \left(\frac {1}{m _ {0}}\right) = \widetilde {\Theta} (1)
$$

Let  $T_{0,i}$  be the first iteration so that  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\geq 2\Lambda_{\emptyset}^{-}$ , and  $T_0\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\Theta \left(\frac{k}{\eta\sigma_0^{q - 2}}\right)$  (noticing  $T_0\geq T_{0,i}$ ) Then,

for every  $i\in [k]$  and  $t\geq T_0$  , it satisfies  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\geq \Lambda_\varnothing^+$

- for every  $i \in [k]$  and  $t \geq T_{0,i}$ , it satisfies  $\Lambda_i^{(t)} \geq \Lambda_\varnothing^-$ .

# D.2.2 Single-View Error Till the End

In this subsection we present a claim to bound the "convergence" (namely, the  $\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_y\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\right)$  part) for every single-view data from  $T_{0}$  till the end.

Claim D.12 (single view till the end). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for all iterations  $t < T$  and  $\gamma \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0 k)$ . We have that

(a) for every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$ , for every  $r\in [m]$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , every  $p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_y,\ell}(X)$

$$
\sum_ {t = T _ {0}} ^ {T} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {y, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {N}{\eta}\right)
$$

(b) for every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$

$$
\sum_ {t = T _ {0}} ^ {T} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right)
$$

Before proving Claim D.12, we first establish a simple claim to bound how the (correlation with the) noise term grows on single view data. This is used to show that the learner learns most single-view data through memorization.

Claim D.13 (noise lower bound). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$ .

(a) For every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , for every  $p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_{y,\ell}}(X)$

$$
\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle - \frac {\eta}{\sqrt {d}} + \widetilde {\Omega} \Big (\frac {\eta}{N} \Big) \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p} \rangle) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X ^ {*})\right) \geq \dots \geq - \frac {\eta T}{\sqrt {d}}
$$

(b) For every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$  , every  $\ell \in [2]$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p \in P _ {v _ {y, \ell}} (X)} \langle w _ {y, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \geq \sum_ {p \in P _ {v _ {y, \ell}} (X)} \langle w _ {y, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle - \frac {O (\eta)}{\sqrt {d}} \\ + \widetilde {\Omega} \Big (\frac {\eta}{N} \Big) \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \Big (\rho \cdot \langle w _ {y, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {y, \ell} \rangle - \widetilde {O} (\eta T / \sqrt {d} + \sigma_ {0} \gamma k) \Big) \Big (1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} (F ^ {(t)}, X) \Big) \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Claim D.13. For every  $(X^{*},y^{*})\in \mathcal{Z}_{s}$ , every  $i\in [k]$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ , and every  $p^*\in P_{v_{i,\ell}}(X^*)$ , one can calculate that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle = \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle + \eta \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(\sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p} \rangle) \langle x _ {p}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right. \\ \left. - \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\sum_ {p \in [ P ]} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p} \rangle\right) \langle x _ {p}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Note when  $X \neq X^{*}$ , we have  $|\langle x_{p},\xi_{p^{*}}\rangle |\leq \widetilde{O} (\sigma_{p})\leq o\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big)$ ; and when  $X = X^{*}$  but  $p \neq p^{*}$ , we also have  $|\langle x_p,\xi_{p^*}\rangle |\leq \widetilde{O} (\sigma_p)\leq o\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big)$ . Therefore, when  $i = y^{*}$

$$
\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle = \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle + \widetilde {\Theta} \left(\frac {\eta}{N}\right) \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p ^ {*}} \rangle) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X ^ {*})\right) \pm \frac {\eta}{\sqrt {d}} \tag {D.6}
$$

Using the non-negativity of  $\widetilde{\mathsf{ReLU}}'$  we arrive at the first conclusion. Next, using Induction Hypothesis C.3d, we have  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_{p^*}\rangle = \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle z_{p^*} + \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},\xi_{p^*}\rangle \pm \widetilde{O} (\sigma_0\gamma k)$ . Also, recall from Def. 3.1 that  $\sum_{p^*\in P_{v_{i,\ell}}(X^*)}z_{p^*}\geq$

$\Omega (\rho)$ . Therefore, when summing over constantly many  $p^* \in P_{v_{i,\ell}}(X^*)$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {p ^ {*} \in P _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X ^ {*})} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle \geq \sum_ {p ^ {*} \in P _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X ^ {*})} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle - \frac {O (\eta)}{\sqrt {d}} \\ + \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {\eta}{N}\right) \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \Big (\rho \cdot \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle - \widetilde {O} (\eta T / \sqrt {d} + \sigma_ {0} \gamma k) \Big) \Big (1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X ^ {*}) \Big) \\ \end{array}
$$

This arrives at our second conclusion.

![](images/9c71a98594de22cd5854ee65723e0f05dcb7752dc7a2811dfe7746ac58c9ed44.jpg)

Proof of Claim D.12. We now prove Claim D.12 using Claim D.13. Let us denote  $i = y$ .

Claim D.12a is in fact a direct corollary of Claim D.13a, because once the summation has reached  $\widetilde{\Omega}\left(\frac{N}{\eta}\right)$  at some iteration  $t = t_0$ , then according to Claim D.13a, we must have already satisfied

$$
\forall t \geq t _ {0}: \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \geq \operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)
$$

but according to  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_p\rangle = \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle z_p + \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},\xi_p\rangle \pm \widetilde{O} (\sigma_0\gamma k)$  from Induction Hypothesis C.3c, and using  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \geq -1$  from Induction Hypothesis C.3h, we immediately have

$$
F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) \geq \left\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p ^ {*}} \right\rangle - O (1) \geq \operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)
$$

while at the same time, one can easily derive (recall (D.3)) that  $F_{j}^{(t)}(X) \leq m_{0} \cdot \Lambda_{i}^{(t)} \cdot \Gamma \leq O(1)$  for every  $j \neq i$ . Therefore, we have  $1 - \mathbf{logit}_y(F^{(t)}, X) \leq e^{-\log^5 k}$  for every  $t \geq t_1$ . This proves the Claim D.12a.

Next, we move to Claim D.12b. We prove by way of contradiction and suppose

$$
\sum_ {t \geq T _ {0}} \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right)
$$

Using  $\Lambda_i^{(t)} = \max_{r\in [m],\ell \in [2]}\left[\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \right]^+\geq \widetilde{\Omega} (1)$  from Claim D.11 and the definition of  $\mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$ , we have

$$
\sum_ {(r, \ell) \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)} \times [ 2 ]} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde {\Omega} (1)} \sum_ {t \geq T _ {0}} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right)
$$

Note that when  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde{\Omega}(1)$  and  $\sum_{p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)} \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, \xi_p \rangle \geq \text{polylog}(k)$  simultaneously hold, there must exist some  $p^* \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)$  so that  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, \xi_{p^*} \rangle \geq \text{polylog}(k)$ , but according to Induction Hypothesis C.3d, we have (noticing  $\widetilde{\text{ReLU}}$  is in the linear regime now because  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle \geq 0$ )

$$
F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, x _ {p ^ {*}} \rangle - O (1) = \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle z _ {p ^ {*}} + \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p ^ {*}} \rangle \pm \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0} \gamma k) - O (1) \geq \mathsf {p o l y l o g} (k)
$$

In contrast, one can derive (recall (D.3)) that  $F_{j}^{(t)}(X) \leq m_{0} \cdot \Lambda_{i}^{(t)} \cdot \Gamma \leq O(1)$  for every  $j \neq i$ . This means  $1 - \mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)}, X) \ll e^{-\log^5 k}$ . In other words,

$$
\sum_ {t \geq T _ {0}} \sum_ {(r, \ell) \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)} \times [ 2 ]} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde {\Omega} (1)} \mathbb {1} _ {\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell} (X)}} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \leq \mathsf {p o l y l o g} (k)} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right)
$$

Now we partition the iterations between  $T_0$  and  $T$  into  $4m_0$  stages of consecutive iterations, denoted

by  $\mathcal{T}_1, \ldots, \mathcal{T}_{4m_0}$ , so that each of them have a similar partial sum in the above summation. In symbols:  $\forall g \in [4m_0]$ :

$$
\sum_ {t \in \mathcal {T} _ {g}} \sum_ {(r, \ell) \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)} \times [ 2 ]} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde {\Omega} (1)} \mathbb {1} _ {\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \leq \operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)} \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right) \tag {D.7}
$$

Let us first look at stage  $\mathcal{T}_1$ . By averaging, there exists some  $(r,\ell) = (r_1^*,\ell_1^*)\in \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}\times [2]$  so that

$$
\sum_ {t \in \mathcal {T} _ {1}} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde {\Omega} (1)} \mathbb {1} _ {\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell} (X)}} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \leq \mathrm {p o l y l o g} (k)} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right)
$$

Applying Claim D.13b, we know that after stage  $\mathcal{T}_1$  (namely, for any  $t\in [\max \mathcal{T}_1,T])$ ), it satisfies

$$
\mathrm {f o r} (r, \ell) = (r _ {1} ^ {*}, \ell_ {1} ^ {*}) \qquad \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right) \cdot \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {\eta}{N} \rho^ {q - 1}\right) > \mathsf {p o l y l o g} (k)
$$

Continuing to stage  $\mathcal{T}_2$ , by averaging again, we can find some other  $(r,\ell) = (r_2^*,\ell_2^*)\in \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}\times [2]$  so that

$$
\sum_ {t \in \mathcal {T} _ {2}} \mathbb {1} _ {\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \widetilde {\Omega} (1)} \mathbb {1} _ {\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell} (X)}} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle \leq \mathsf {p o l y l o g} (k)} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \geq \widetilde {\Omega} \left(\frac {N}{\eta \rho^ {q - 1}}\right)
$$

From the conclusion of the previous stage, it must satisfy that  $(r_2^*,\ell_2^*)\neq (r_1^*,\ell_1^*)$ . A similar derivation also tells us that after stage  $\mathcal{T}_2$  (namely, for any  $t\in [\max \mathcal{T}_2,T]$ ), it satisfies

$$
\text {f o r} (r, \ell) = \left(r _ {2} ^ {*}, \ell_ {2} ^ {*}\right) \quad \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle > \operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)
$$

We continue this argument until we finish stage  $\mathcal{T}_{2m_0}$ . At this point, we know for every  $t\in$ $[\max \mathcal{T}_{2m_0},T]$

$$
\text {f o r a l l} (r, \ell) \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)} \times [ 2 ] \quad \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, \xi_ {p} \rangle > \operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)
$$

This contradicts (D.7) for any  $g > 2m_0$ .

# D.2.3 Multi-View Error Till the End

In this subsection we present a claim to bound the "convergence" (namely, the  $\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_y\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\right)$  part) for the average multi-view data from  $T_{0}$  till the end.

Claim D.14 (multi-view till the end). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for every iteration  $t < T$ , and suppose  $N_{s} \leq \frac{k^{2}\rho^{q - 1}}{s}$ , then

$$
\sum_ {t = T _ {0}} ^ {T} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ 1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {k}{\eta}\right) + \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {s N _ {s}}{\eta k \rho^ {q - 1}}\right) \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {k}{\eta}\right)
$$

In fact, Claim D.14 is a direct corollary of the following claim, combined with  $\Lambda_i^{(t)} = \widetilde{O}(1)$  from Induction Hypothesis C.3g, and with the convergence Claim D.12b for single-view data.

Claim D.15. Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds at iteration  $t$  and  $t \geq T_0$ , then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in [ k ]} \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} \geq \sum_ {i \in [ k ]} \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)} + \Omega (\eta) \times \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ 1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ - \eta O \left(\frac {s}{k} \frac {N _ {s}}{N}\right) _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \mathbb {E} \left[ 1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Claim D.15. Recall  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\max_{r\in [m],\ell \in [2]}[\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle ]^+$ . Let us take  $r,\ell$  to be this argmax so that Claim D.11 tells us  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \geq \Lambda_{\emptyset}^{+} = \widetilde{\Theta} (1)$ . By Claim D.7 and Claim D.8,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) - \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P)\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right. \\ \left. - \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} V _ {i, r, \ell} (X)\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) = \sum_{p\in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)}\widetilde{\mathsf{ReLU}}^{\prime}(\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},x_p\rangle)z_p$ . Using Induction Hypothesis C.3a, we know that as long as  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_m$ , or when  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$  but  $i\neq y$ , it satisfies

$$
V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) = \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathrm {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} \left(\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle z _ {p} \pm \widetilde {o} (\sigma_ {0})\right) z _ {p}
$$

Since  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle \geq \Lambda_{\emptyset}^{+} = \Theta \left(\frac{1}{m_0}\right) \gg \varrho$  (see Claim D.11) and since  $|\mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)| \leq O(1)$ , for most of  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}$  we must be already in the linear regime of  $\widetilde{\text{ReLU}}$  so

$$
0. 9 \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} z _ {p} \leq V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) \leq \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} z _ {p}
$$

According to our choice of the distribution (see Def. 3.1):

- When  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$  and  $y = i$ , we have  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) \geq 0.9$ .  
- When  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_s$  and  $y = i$ , we have  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) \geq 0$ .  
- When  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$ ,  $y \neq i$  and  $v_{i,\ell} \in \mathcal{P}(X)$ , we have  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) \leq 0.4$ .  
- When  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_s$ ,  $y \neq i$  and  $v_{i,\ell} \in \mathcal{P}(X)$ , we have  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X) \leq \Gamma \leq 1$ .

Together, we derive that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ 0. 8 9 \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right] \\ - \eta_ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + 0. 4 \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)}\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ - O \left(\frac {\eta N _ {s}}{N}\right) _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \mathbb {E} \left[ \mathbf {1} _ {y = i} \cdot \widetilde {O} \left(\sigma_ {p} P\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \\ - O \left(\frac {\eta N _ {s}}{k N}\right) _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)}\right) \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \tag {D.8} \\ \end{array}
$$

Above, we have applied Claim D.4 which says for  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$ , it holds that  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X)\leq O\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_y(F^{(t)},X)\right)$ .

Finally, substituting  $\mathbb{1}_{v_{i,\ell}\in \mathcal{P}(X)}$  with the naive upper bound  $\mathcal{E}_1 + \mathcal{E}_3 + 0.4\mathbb{1}_{v_{i,\ell}\in \mathcal{P}(X)}\leq 0.41$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \geq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \mathbb {E} \left[ 0. 8 9 \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) - 0. 4 1 \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \left. \right. - O \left(\frac {\eta N _ {s}}{k N}\right) _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \left[\left(k \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \cdot \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P) + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)}\right)\right)\left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right)\right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Summing up over all  $i \in [k]$ , and using  $v_{i,\ell} \in \mathcal{P}(X)$  with probability  $\frac{s}{k}$  when  $i \neq y$ , we finish the proof.

# D.2.4 Multi-View Individual Error

Our next claim states that up to a polynomial factor, the error on any individual multi-view data is bounded by the training error.

Claim D.16 (multi-view individual error). For every  $t \geq 0$ , every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$ ,

$$
1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {k ^ {4}}{s ^ {2}}\right) \cdot \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ 1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right]
$$

(The same also holds w.p.  $\geq 1 - e^{-\Omega (\log^2 k)}$  for every  $(X,y)\sim \mathcal{D}_m$  on the left hand side.)

Furthermore, if  $\mathbb{E}_{(X,y)\sim \mathcal{Z}_m}\left[1 - \mathbf{logit}_y\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\right]\leq \frac{1}{k^4}$  is sufficiently small, we have  $0.4\Phi_i^{(t)} - \Phi_j^{(t)}\leq -\Omega (\log k)$  for every pair  $i,j\in [k]$ .

Proof. For a data point  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$ , let us denote by  $\mathcal{H}(X)$  be the set of all  $i \in [k] \setminus \{y\}$  such that,

$$
\sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} z _ {p} \geq 0. 8 - \frac {1}{1 0 0 \log (k)}, \quad \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {y, \ell}} (X)} z _ {p} \leq 2 + \frac {1}{1 0 0 \log (k)}
$$

Now, suppose  $1 - \mathbf{logit}_y(F^{(t)},X) = \xi (X)$ , then using  $\min \{1,\beta \} \leq 2\bigl (1 - \frac{1}{1 + \beta}\bigr)$ , we have

$$
\min  \left\{1, \sum_ {i \in [ k ] \backslash \{y \}} e ^ {F _ {i} ^ {(t)} (X) - F _ {y} ^ {(t)} (X)} \right\} \leq 2 \xi (X)
$$

By Claim D.9 and our definition of  $\mathcal{H}(X)$ , this implies that

$$
\min  \left\{1, \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {H} (X)} e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {y} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \leq 4 \xi (X)
$$

If we denote by  $\psi = \mathbb{E}_{(X,y)\sim \mathcal{Z}_m}\left[1 - \mathbf{logit}_y\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\right]$ , then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \min \left\{1, \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {H} (X)} e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {y} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \right] \leq 4 \psi \\ \Rightarrow \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {H} (X)} \min \left\{\frac {1}{k}, e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {y} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \right] \leq 4 \psi \\ \end{array}
$$

Notice that we can rewrite the LHS so that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \sum_ {j \in [ k ]} \mathbb {1} _ {j = y} \sum_ {i \in [ k ]} \mathbb {1} _ {i \in \mathcal {H} (X)} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{k}, e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {j} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \right] \leq 4 \psi \\ \Rightarrow \sum_ {j \in [ k ]} \sum_ {i \in [ k ]} \mathbb {1} _ {i \neq y} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {j = y} \mathbb {1} _ {i \in \mathcal {H} (X)} \right] \min  \left\{\frac {1}{k}, e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {j} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \leq 4 \psi \\ \end{array}
$$

Note however, that for every  $i \neq j \in [k]$ , the probability of generating a multi-view sample  $(X,y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$  with  $y = j$  and  $i \in \mathcal{H}(X)$  is at least  $\widetilde{\Omega}\left(\frac{1}{k} \cdot \frac{s^2}{k^2}\right)$ . This implies

$$
\sum_ {j \in [ k ]} \sum_ {i \in [ k ] \backslash i} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{k}, e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {j} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {k ^ {3}}{s ^ {2}} \psi\right) \tag {D.9}
$$

Finally, using  $1 - \frac{1}{1 + \beta} \leq \min \{1, \beta\}$ , it is easy to see for every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$

$$
1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) = \min  \left\{1, \sum_ {i \in [ k ] \backslash \{y \}} 2 e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {y} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \leq k \cdot \sum_ {i \in [ k ] \backslash \{y \}} \min  \left\{\frac {1}{k}, e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {y} ^ {(t)}} \right\} \leq \widetilde {O} \Big (\frac {k ^ {4}}{s ^ {2}} \psi \Big)
$$

We complete the proof.

Note that if one replaces  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_m$  with  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{D}_m$ , we also have

$$
1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) = \min \left\{1, \sum_ {i \in [ k ] \backslash \{y \}} 2 e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)} - \Phi_ {y} ^ {(t)}} \right\}
$$

with high probability, so the same result also holds.

Note also (D.9) implies if  $\mathbb{E}_{(X,y)\sim \mathcal{Z}_m}\left[1 - \mathbf{logit}_y\left(F^{(t)},X\right)\right]\leq \frac{1}{k^4}$  is sufficiently small, we have  $0.4\Phi_{i}^{(t)} - \Phi_{j}^{(t)}\leq -\Omega (\log k)$  for every pair  $i\neq j$  . Using the non-negativity of  $\Phi_i^{(t)}$  , we know the relationship holds also when  $i = j$

![](images/1c017cf4689467902b606ed5454b2c0f7a3bb2f6fbb53461ff4e307cf160ca99.jpg)

# D.2.5 Multi-View Error in Stage 2

As we shall see later, our final proof is divided into three stages for each index  $i \in [k]$ : the first stage is for  $t \leq T_{0,i}$ , the second stage is for all  $t \in [T_{0,i}, T_0]$ , and the third iteration is for  $t > T_0$ . We have the following claim to bound the maximum error of multi-view data during the second stage.

Claim D.17 (multi-view stage 2). Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for every iteration  $t \leq T_0$ , and  $\Upsilon = \widetilde{\Theta} \left( \frac{1}{k^{0.2}} \right)$  is a parameter. Then, for every  $i \in [k]$

(a)

$$
\sum_ {t = T _ {0, i}} ^ {T _ {0}} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \mathbf {1} _ {y = i} \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \leq O \left(\frac {s}{k} T _ {0} \Upsilon\right) + \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {1}{\eta}\right)
$$

(b) for every  $t \in [T_{0,i}, T_0]$ , every  $j \in [k] \setminus \{i\}$ , every  $\ell \in [2]$ ,

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X) \right] \leq O (\frac {1}{k})
$$

$$
\mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \leq O (\frac {s}{k ^ {2}})
$$

In order to prove Claim D.17 we first establish the following claim.

Claim D.18. Let  $\Upsilon$  be any  $\Upsilon \in \left[\frac{1}{k},\frac{1}{s}\right]$ , and recall  $\Phi_i^{(t)} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sum_{r \in [m], \ell \in [2]} [\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle]^{+}$  from (D.4). Then, letting  $T_1 \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{k^{2.5}\Upsilon^{2.5}}{\eta}\right)$  and suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for all iterations  $t \leq T_1$ . Then,

$$
\forall t \leq T _ {1} , \quad \forall i \in [ k ]: \quad e ^ {0. 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)}} \leq k \Upsilon .
$$

(Note when  $\Upsilon \leq \widetilde{O}(k^{-0.2})$  we have  $T_0 \leq T_1$ .)

Proof of Claim D.18. Recall from Induction Hypothesis C.3i that for those  $r \in [m] \setminus \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$  and  $\ell \in [2]$ , it satisfies  $[\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle]^{+} \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$  and their summation does not exceed  $\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  due to our choice of  $m$ . Thus, to prove this claim, it suffices to slightly abuse the notation and think of

$$
\widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)} = \max _ {i \in [ k ]} \sum_ {(r, \ell) \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)} \times [ 2 ]} [ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle ] ^ {+}
$$

Let  $i$  be this  $\arg \max$  in  $\widehat{\Phi}^{(t)}$ . For every  $(r,\ell)\in \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}\times [2]$ , by Claim D.7 and Claim D.8,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \leq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {\mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3}\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right. \\ \left. + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\widetilde {O} \left(\sigma_ {p} P\right)\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \tag {D.10} \\ \end{array}
$$

where recall

$$
V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) = \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) z _ {p} \leq \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} z _ {p} \leq O (1)
$$

Therefore,

$$
\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \leq \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + O (\eta) \Big (\underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \big (1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} (F ^ {(t)}, X) \big) \right] + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P) \Big)
$$

Note that single-view data contribute to at most  $O\left(\frac{\eta N_s}{kN}\right)$  on the RHS of (D.10), so we only focus on those  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$ . By Claim D.9 we know  $F_y^{(t)}(X) \geq \Phi_y^{(t)} - \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  and for  $j \in [k] \setminus \{y\}$ ,

- W.p.  $1 - (1 - \frac{s}{k})^2$ , both  $v_{j,1}, v_{j,2} \notin \mathcal{P}(X)$ , and in this case  $F_j^{(t)}(X) \leq \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ .  
- W.p.  $(1 - \frac{s}{k})^2$ , at least one of  $v_{j,1}, v_{j,2} \in \mathcal{P}(X)$ , and in this case  $F_j^{(t)}(X) \leq 0.4\Phi_j^{(t)} + \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ ; Together, and using the inequality  $1 - \mathbf{logit}_y(F^{(t)}, X) \leq \frac{\sum_{j \neq y} e^{F_j^{(t)}(X)}}{e^{F_y^{(t)}(X)}}$ , and conclude that

$$
\underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {i = y} \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \leq \frac {1}{k} \cdot O \left(\frac {s e ^ {0 . 4 \widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)}} + k}{e ^ {\widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)}}}\right)
$$

Summing up over all  $(r,\ell)\in \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}\times [2]$  with  $|\mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}|\leq m_0\leq \widetilde{O} (1)$ , we have

$$
\widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t + 1)} \leq \widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)} + \eta \frac {1}{k} \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {s e ^ {0 . 4 \widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)}} + k}{e ^ {\widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)}}} + \frac {N _ {s}}{N}\right)
$$

This implies whenever  $e^{\widehat{\Phi} (t)} = \Omega \left(k^{2.5}\Upsilon^{2.5}\right)$ , we have

$$
\widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t + 1)} \leq \widehat {\Phi} ^ {(t)} + \eta \widetilde {O} \Big (\frac {1}{k ^ {2 . 5} \Upsilon^ {2 . 5}} \Big)
$$

This finishes the proof of Claim D.18.

![](images/9a429689aa1eb8e8c145f3c332e9256985592d50761dededae07436e3ca95ad8.jpg)

Proof of Claim D.17. We first prove Claim D.17a and the proof of Claim D.17b is only simpler. Using (D.8) in the proof of Claim D.15, we know as long as  $t \geq T_{0,i}$  (so  $\Lambda_i^{(t)} \geq \Lambda_\emptyset$ ),<sup>17</sup>

$$
\begin{array}{l} \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} \geq \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)} + \eta \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \widetilde {\Omega} (1) \cdot \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right] \\ - \eta \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} (\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + 0. 4 \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, 1} \mathrm {o r} v _ {i, 2} \in \mathcal {P} (X)}) \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \left. - O \left(\frac {\eta N _ {s}}{N}\right) \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \cdot \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \right. \\ - O \Big (\frac {\eta N _ {s}}{k N} \Big) \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, 1} \text {o r} v _ {i, 2} \in \mathcal {P} (X)}\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying Claim D.9, we have for  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_m$  and  $i\neq y$ , if at least one of  $\{v_{i,1},v_{i,2}\}$  is in  $\mathcal{P}(X)$ , then  $F_{i}^{(t)}(X)\leq 0.4\Phi_{i}^{(t)} + \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ . Therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, 1} \text {o r} v _ {i, 2} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \leq \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, 1} \mathrm {o r} v _ {i, 2} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} \frac {1}{1 + \sum_ {j \in [ k ]} e ^ {F _ {j} ^ {(t)} (X) - 0 . 4 \Phi_ {i} ^ {(t)}}} \right] \\ \leq \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {i, 1} \text {o r} v _ {i, 2} \in \mathcal {P} (X)} O (\Upsilon) \right] \leq O \left(\frac {s \Upsilon}{k}\right) \tag {D.11} \\ \end{array}
$$

Above, the last inequality uses  $F_{j}^{(t)}(X) \geq 0$  and Claim D.18 which says  $e^{0.4\Phi_i^{(t)}} \leq O(k\Upsilon)$ . Also, for obvious reason

$$
\underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \left(k \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \cdot \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P) + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathbb {1} _ {\{v _ {i, 1}, v _ {i, 2} \} \in \mathcal {P} (X)}\right)\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \leq O (\frac {s}{k})
$$

Together, we arrive at the conclusion that

$$
\Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t + 1)} \geq \Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)} + \Omega (\eta) \Big (\underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right] - O \left(\frac {s \Upsilon}{k} + \frac {s}{k ^ {2}} \frac {N _ {s}}{N}\right) \Big)
$$

Using  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\leq \widetilde{O} (1)$  from Induction Hypothesis C.3g we immediate finish the proof of Claim D.17a.

As for the first part of Claim D.17b, note that when  $v_{i,1} \notin P(x)$  and  $v_{i,2} \notin P(x)$ , we have  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X) \leq O(\frac{1}{k})$ ; but if one of them belongs to  $P(X)$  the probability is  $\frac{s}{k}$  and we have (D.11). They together (and using  $s\Upsilon \leq 1$ ) imply the first part of Claim D.17b.

As for the second part of Claim D.17b, it is similar to the first part once we take into account  $\mathbf{Pr}[v_{j,\ell} \in \mathcal{P}(X)] = \frac{s}{k}$ .

# D.3 Tensor Power Method Bound

In this subsection we establish a lemma for comparing the growth speed of two sequences of updates of the form  $x_{t + 1} \gets x_t + \eta C_t x_t^{q - 1}$ . This should be reminiscent of the classical analysis of the growth of eigenvalues on the (incremental) tensor power method of degree  $q$ .

Lemma D.19. Let  $q \geq 3$  be a constant and  $x_0, y_0 = o(1)$ . Let  $\{x_t, y_t\}_{t \geq 0}$  be two positive sequences updated as

-  $x_{t+1} \geq x_t + \eta C_t x_t^{q-1}$  for some  $C_t = \Theta(1)$ , and  
-  $y_{t + 1} \leq y_t + \eta SC_t y_t^{q - 1}$  for some constant  $S = \Theta(1)$ .

Suppose  $x_0 \geq y_0 S^{\frac{1}{q - 2}} (1 + \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)})$ , then we must have for every  $A = O(1)$ , let  $T_x$  be the first iteration such that  $x_t \geq A$ , then

$$
y _ {T _ {x}} \leq O \left(y _ {0} \cdot \operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)\right)
$$

We first establish a claim before proving Lemma D.19.

Claim D.20. Consider an increasing sequence  $x_{t} \geq 0$  defined as  $x_{t+1} = x_{t} + \eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q-1}$  for some  $C_{t} = \Theta(1)$ , then we have for every  $A > x_{0}$ , every  $\delta \in (0,1)$ , and every  $\eta \in (0,1)$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t \geq 0, x _ {t} \leq A} \eta C _ {t} \geq \left[ \frac {\delta (1 + \delta) ^ {- 1}}{(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2} - 1} \left(1 - \left(\frac {(1 + \delta) x _ {0}}{A}\right) ^ {q - 2}\right) - \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0}} \frac {\log \left(\frac {A}{x _ {0}}\right)}{\log (1 + \delta)} \right] \cdot \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} \\ \sum_ {t \geq 0, x _ {t} \leq A} \eta C _ {t} \leq \left[ \frac {(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2}}{(q - 2)} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0}} \frac {\log \left(\frac {A}{x _ {0}}\right)}{\log (1 + \delta)} \right] \cdot \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Claim D.20. For every  $g = 0,1,2,\ldots$ , let  $\mathcal{T}_g$  be the first iteration such that  $x_{t} \geq (1 + \delta)^{g}x_{0}$ . Let  $b$  be the smallest integer such that  $(1 + \delta)^{b}x_{0} \geq A$ . Suppose for notation simplicity that we replace  $x_{t}$  with exactly  $A$  whenever  $x_{t} \geq A$ .

By the definition of  $\mathcal{T}_g$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t \in \left[ \mathcal {T} _ {g}, \mathcal {T} _ {g + 1}\right)} \eta C _ {t} [ (1 + \delta) ^ {g} x _ {0} ] ^ {(q - 1)} \leq x _ {\mathcal {T} _ {g + 1}} - x _ {\mathcal {T} _ {g}} \leq \delta (1 + \delta) ^ {g} x _ {0} + O (\eta A ^ {q - 1}) \\ \sum_ {t \in [ \mathcal {T} _ {g}, \mathcal {T} _ {g + 1})} \eta C _ {t} [ (1 + \delta) ^ {g + 1} x _ {0} ] ^ {(q - 1)} \geq x _ {\mathcal {T} _ {g + 1}} - x _ {\mathcal {T} _ {g}} \geq \delta (1 + \delta) ^ {g} x _ {0} - O (\eta A ^ {q - 1}) \\ \end{array}
$$

These imply that

$$
\sum_ {t \in [ \mathcal {T} _ {g}, \mathcal {T} _ {g + 1})} \eta C _ {t} \leq \frac {\delta}{(1 + \delta) ^ {g (q - 2)}} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}}
$$

$$
\sum_ {t \in [ \mathcal {T} _ {g}, \mathcal {T} _ {g + 1})} \eta C _ {t} \geq \frac {\delta}{(1 + \delta) ^ {g (q - 2)} (1 + \delta) ^ {q - 1}} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} - \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}}
$$

Recall  $b$  is the smallest integer such that  $(1 + \delta)^{b}x_{0} \geq A$ , so we can calculate

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t \geq 0, x _ {t} \leq A} \eta C _ {t} \leq \sum_ {g = 0} ^ {b - 1} \frac {\delta}{(1 + \delta) ^ {g (q - 2)}} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b = \frac {\delta}{1 - \frac {1}{(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2}}} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b \\ = \frac {\delta (1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2}}{(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2} - 1} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b \leq \frac {(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2}}{(q - 2)} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t \geq 0, x _ {t} \leq A} \eta C _ {t} \geq \sum_ {q = 0} ^ {b - 2} \frac {\delta}{(1 + \delta) ^ {g (q - 2)} (1 + \delta) ^ {q - 1}} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} - \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b \\ \geq \frac {\delta (1 + \delta) ^ {- 1} \left(1 - \frac {1}{(1 + \delta) ^ {(q - 2) (b - 1)}}\right)}{(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2} - 1} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} - \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b \\ \geq \frac {\delta (1 + \delta) ^ {- 1} \left(1 - \left(\frac {(1 + \delta) x _ {0}}{A}\right) ^ {q - 2}\right)}{(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2} - 1} \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} - \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} b \\ \end{array}
$$

Proof of Lemma D.19. Let us apply Claim D.20 twice, once for the  $x_{t}$  sequence with  $C_t$  and threshold  $A$ , and the other time for the  $y_{t}$  sequence with  $C_t' = SC_t$  and threshold  $A' = y_0 \cdot \mathrm{polylog}(k)$ . Let  $T_{x}$  be the first iteration  $t$  in which  $x_{t} \geq A$ , and  $T_{y}$  be the first iteration  $t$  in which  $y_{t} \geq A'$ .

According to Claim D.20, we know

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {T _ {x}} \eta C _ {t} \leq \left[ \frac {(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2}}{(q - 2)} + \frac {O (\eta A ^ {q - 1})}{x _ {0}} \frac {\log \left(\frac {A}{x _ {0}}\right)}{\log (1 + \delta)} \right] \cdot \frac {1}{x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} \\ \leq \frac {1 + O (\delta)}{(q - 2) x _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} + O \Big (\frac {\eta \log (1 / x _ {0})}{\delta x _ {0} ^ {q - 1}} \Big) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {T _ {y}} \eta C _ {t} ^ {\prime} = \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {T _ {y}} \eta S C _ {t} \geq \left[ \frac {\delta (1 + \delta) ^ {- 1}}{(1 + \delta) ^ {q - 2} - 1} \left(1 - \left(\frac {(1 + \delta) y _ {0}}{A ^ {\prime}}\right) ^ {q - 2}\right) - \frac {O (\eta (A ^ {\prime}) ^ {q - 1})}{y _ {0}} \frac {\log \left(\frac {A ^ {\prime}}{y _ {0}}\right)}{\log (1 + \delta)} \right] \cdot \frac {1}{y _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} \\ \geq \frac {1 - O (\delta + \frac {1}{\mathrm {p o l y l o g} (k)})}{(q - 2) y _ {0} ^ {q - 2}} - \widetilde {O} \Big (\frac {\eta}{\delta} \Big) \\ \end{array}
$$

Therefore, choosing  $\delta = \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  and  $\eta \leq \frac{x_0}{\log(1 / x_0)\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ , together with the assumption  $x_0 \geq y_0 S^{\frac{1}{q - 2}}(1 + \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)})$ , we immediately have that  $T_x < T_y$  so we finish the proof.

# D.4 Main Lemmas for Proving the Induction Hypothesis

In this subsection, we begin to provide key technical lemmas that, when combined together, shall prove Induction Hypothesis C.3. (We combine the analysis in the next subsection, Section D.5.)

# D.4.1 Lambda Lemma

Recall  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\max_{r\in [m],\ell \in [2]}\left[\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \right]^+$ . Our first lemma shows that  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}$  cannot go above  $\widetilde{O}(1)$ .

Lemma D.21. Suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for all iterations  $< t$  and suppose  $N_s \leq \widetilde{o}(k / \rho)$ . Then, letting  $\Phi_{i,\ell}^{(t)} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sum_{r \in [m]} [\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle]^{+}$ , we have

$$
\forall i \in [ k ], \forall \ell \in [ 2 ]: \qquad \Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \leq \widetilde {O} (1)
$$

This implies  $\Lambda_i^{(t)}\leq \widetilde{O} (1)$  as well.

Proof of Lemma D.21. We make a simple observation:

- For those  $r \in [m] \setminus \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}$  and  $\ell \in [2]$ , recall Induction Hypothesis C.3i says  $[\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle]^{+} \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)$  and their summation does not exceed  $\frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  due to our choice of  $m$ .

Therefore, to prove this claim, it suffices to slightly abuse the notation and prove  $\overline{\Phi}_{i,\ell}^{(t)}\leq \widetilde{O} (1)$  for

$$
\overline {{\Phi}} _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {r \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)}} \left[ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right] ^ {+} = \Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \pm \frac {1}{\mathrm {p o l y} (k)}
$$

Recall gradient descent update gives

$$
\langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle = \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle + \eta_ {\mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}}} \left[ \langle - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F ^ {(t)}; X, y), v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right]
$$

when taking the positive part, we know there exists  $\Delta_{i,r,\ell}^{(t)}\in [0,1]$  such that

$$
\left[ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right] ^ {+} = \left[ \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right] ^ {+} + \eta \Delta_ {i, r, \ell} ^ {(t)} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \langle - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F ^ {(t)}; X, y), v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right]
$$

Now in each iteration  $t$ , for every pair  $(i,\ell)$ , we define a special subset  $\mathcal{Z}_{s,i,\ell}$  of the single-view data as

$$
\mathcal {Z} _ {s, i, \ell} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \left\{\left(X, y\right) \in \mathcal {Z} _ {s} \mid y = i \wedge \widehat {\ell} (X) = 3 - \ell \right\}
$$

For analysis purpose, from iteration  $t = 0$  onwards, we define two sequences of quantities

define  $A_{i,\ell}^{(0)}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\sum_{r\in \mathcal{M}_i^{(0)}}\left[\langle w_{i,r}^{(0)},v_{i,\ell}\rangle \right]^+$  and  $B_{i,\ell}^{(0)} = 0$  
- when  $t \geq 0$ , define

$$
A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t + 1)} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} + \eta \sum_ {r \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)}} \Delta_ {i, r, \ell} ^ {(t)} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {(X, y) \notin \mathcal {Z} _ {s, i, \ell}} \cdot \langle - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F ^ {(t)}; X, y), v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right]
$$

$$
B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t + 1)} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} + \eta \sum_ {r \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)}} \Delta_ {i, r, \ell} ^ {(t)} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {(X, y) \in \mathcal {Z} _ {s, i, \ell}} \cdot \langle - \nabla_ {w _ {i, r}} L (F ^ {(t)}; X, y), v _ {i, \ell} \rangle \right]
$$

For obvious reason, we have  $\overline{\Phi}_{i,\ell}^{(t)} = A_{i,\ell}^{(t)} + B_{i,\ell}^{(t)}$ .

Bound the  $B$  sequence. Applying Claim D.7, we have

$$
B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t + 1)} = B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} + \eta \sum_ {r \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)}} \Delta_ {i, r, \ell} ^ {(t)} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {(X, y) \in \mathcal {Z} _ {s, i, \ell}} \cdot (V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) \pm (\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3})) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \right]
$$

but according to the definition of  $\mathcal{Z}_{s,i,\ell}$  (which implies  $\widehat{\ell}(X) = 3 - \ell$ ), we must have

$$
0 \leq V _ {i, r, \ell} (X) = \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle) z _ {p} \leq O (\rho) \cdot \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\operatorname {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle)
$$

This means, we must have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t + 1)} - B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \right| \\ \leq O \Big (\frac {\eta \rho N _ {s}}{N} \Big) \sum_ {r \in \mathcal {M} _ {i} ^ {(0)}} \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {(X, y) \in \mathcal {Z} _ {s, i, \ell}} \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} (F ^ {(t)}, X)\right) \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {i, \ell}} (X)} \widetilde {\mathsf {R e L U}} ^ {\prime} (\langle w _ {i, r}, x _ {p} \rangle)\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Applying Claim D.12a (and noticing  $T_0 \leq O\left(\frac{N}{\eta}\right)$ ), we conclude that (using our parameter assumption)

$$
\forall t \geq 0: \quad | B _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} | \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\rho N _ {s}}{k}\right) <   \frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)}
$$

Bound the  $A$  sequence. So far we have derived that  $\Phi_{i,\ell}^{(t)} = A_{i,\ell}^{(t)}\pm \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$  (because  $|B_{i,\ell}^{(t)}|$ ,  $|\Phi_{i,\ell}^{(t)}-\overline{\Phi}_{i,\ell}^{(t)}| \leq \frac{1}{\mathrm{polylog}(k)}$ ). So, it suffices to bound the  $A$  sequence. Let us denote by

$$
\Phi^ {(t)} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \max  _ {i \in [ k ], \ell \in [ 2 ]} \Phi_ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)}
$$

in the remainder of the proof. Suppose we are now at some iteration  $t \geq T_0$ , let

$$
(i, \ell) = \arg \max _ {i \in [ k ], \ell \in [ 2 ]} \left\{A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \right\}
$$

Applying Claim D.7 and Claim D.8, and using  $V_{i,r,\ell}(X)\in [0,1]$ , we have

$$
A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t + 1)} \leq A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} + O (\eta) \Big (\underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \mathbb {1} _ {(X, y) \notin \mathcal {Z} _ {s, i, \ell}} \big (1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} (F ^ {(t)}, X) \big) \right] + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {p} P) \Big)
$$

Observe that, whenever  $A_{i,\ell}^{(t)} > \mathsf{polylog}(k)$ , we also have  $\Phi^{(t)} \geq \mathsf{polylog}(k)$  and thus

- for every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_m$  with  $y = i$ , recall from Claim D.9 that

$$
F _ {j} ^ {(t)} (X) = \sum_ {\ell \in [ 2 ]} \left(\Phi_ {j, \ell} ^ {(t)} \times \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)} \left(\sum_ {p \in \mathcal {P} _ {v _ {j, \ell}} (X)} z _ {p}\right)\right) \pm O (\frac {1}{\operatorname {p o l y l o g} (k)})
$$

By our choice of the distribution, this implies

-  $F_{j}^{(t)}(X)\leq 0.8001\Phi^{(t)}$  for  $j\neq i$  , and  
-  $F_{i}^{(t)}(X)\geq 0.9999\Phi^{(t)}$  because  $(i,\ell)$  is the argmax of  $A_{i,\ell}^{(t)}$  and  $A_{i,\ell}^{(t)}$  is close to  $\Phi_{i,\ell}^{(t)}$ .

- for every  $(X, y) \in \mathcal{Z}_s$  with  $y = i$  and  $\widehat{\ell}(X) = \ell$ , we can also use Claim D.9 to derive

-  $F_{j}^{(t)}(X) \leq 0.8001\Phi^{(t)}$  for  $j \neq i$ .  
However, on the  $i$ -th output, for every  $p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)$ , using  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, x_p \rangle = \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle z_p + \langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, \xi_p \rangle \pm \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0 \gamma k)$  from Induction Hypothesis C.3d and using  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, \xi_p \rangle \geq -\frac{1}{\text{polylog}(k)}$  from Claim D.13a, and  $\sum_{p \in \mathcal{P}_{v_{i,\ell}}(X)} z_p \geq 1$  from Def. 3.1, we also have  
-  $F_{i}^{(t)}(X)\geq \Phi_{i,\ell}^{(t)} - 0.0001\geq 0.9999\Phi^{(t)}$

In both cases, we have  $1 - \mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X) = e^{-\Omega (\log^5 k)}$  which is negligible. Therefore, we derived that

$$
\mathrm {w h e n e v e r} \max _ {i \in [ k ], \ell \in [ 2 ]} \left\{A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \right\} \geq \mathsf {p o l y l o g} (k) \quad \max _ {i \in [ k ], \ell \in [ 2 ]} \left\{A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t + 1)} \right\} \leq \max _ {i \in [ k ], \ell \in [ 2 ]} \left\{A _ {i, \ell} ^ {(t)} \right\} + \widetilde {O} \big (\eta \cdot e ^ {- \Omega (\log^ {5} k)} + \eta \sigma_ {p} P \big) .
$$

This finishes the proof that all  $A_{i,\ell}^{(t)}\leq \widetilde{O} (1)$

# D.4.2 Off-Diagonal Correlations are Small

Our previous subsection upper bounds the "diagonal" correlations  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{i,\ell} \rangle$ , and in this subsection we bound the "off-diagonal" correlations  $\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{j,\ell} \rangle$  for  $i \neq j$ .

Lemma D.22. Suppose Parameter  $D.1$  holds and suppose Induction Hypothesis C.3 holds for all iterations  $< t$ . Then,

$$
\forall i \in [ k ], \forall r \in [ m ], \forall j \in [ k ] \setminus \{i \}: | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | \leq \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0})
$$

Proof of Lemma D.22. Let us denote by  $R_{i}^{(t)} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \max_{r \in [m], j \in [k] \setminus \{i\}} |\langle w_{i,r}^{(t)}, v_{j,\ell} \rangle|$ .

By Claim D.7 and Claim D.8,

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | + \eta_ {\mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {2, i, r} (X) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathcal {E} _ {4, j, \ell} (X)\right) \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right. \\ \left. + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \mathcal {E} _ {4, j, \ell} (X)\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Stage 1. In the first stage, namely when  $t \leq T_{0,i}$ , we have  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X) \leq O(\frac{1}{k})$  (see Claim D.4), have  $\mathcal{E}_{2,i,r}(X) \leq \widetilde{O}\big(\gamma (\Lambda_i^{(t)})^{q - 1}\big)$ , and have  $\mathcal{E}_{4,i,r}(X) \leq \widetilde{O} (\sigma_0)^{q - 1}\mathbb{1}_{v_j,\ell \in \mathcal{V}(X)}$ , so

$$
\left| \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle \right| \leq \left| \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle \right| + \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {\eta}{k}\right) \left(\gamma \left(\Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)}\right) ^ {q - 1} + \left(\sigma_ {0} ^ {q - 1}\right) \gamma s + \widetilde {O} \left((\sigma_ {0} \gamma k) ^ {q - 1}\right) \gamma P + (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \frac {s}{k}\right) \tag {D.12}
$$

Recall in the first stage  $\Lambda_{i}^{(t + 1)} = \Lambda_{i}^{(t)} + \Theta \left(\frac{\eta}{k}\right)\widetilde{\mathsf{ReL}}\mathsf{U}'(\Lambda_{i}^{(t)})$  (see Claim D.10) and therefore

$$
\sum_ {t \leq T _ {0, i}} \eta \left(\Lambda_ {i} ^ {(t)}\right) ^ {q - 1} \leq \widetilde {O} (k)
$$

Hence, together with  $T_{0,i} \leq T_0 = \Theta \left( \frac{k}{\eta \sigma_0^{q - 2}} \right)$  (see Claim D.11), as long as

$$
\gamma = \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}), \quad \gamma = \widetilde {O} (1 / s), \quad (\gamma k) ^ {q - 1} \gamma P = \widetilde {O} (1) \tag {D.13}
$$

(Note that all of them are satisfied by Parameter D.1). Plugging them back to (D.12), we have we have

$$
R _ {i} ^ {(t)} \leq R _ {i} ^ {(0)} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) + \widetilde {O} \Big (\frac {\eta}{k} T _ {0} \Big) \left((\sigma_ {0} ^ {q - 1}) \gamma s + \widetilde {O} \left((\sigma_ {0} \gamma k) ^ {q - 1}\right) \gamma P + (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \frac {s}{k}\right) \leq \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0})
$$

for every  $t \leq T_{0,i}$ .

Stage 2. In the second stage, namely when  $t \in [T_{0,i}, T_0]$ , we have the naive upper bounds  $\mathcal{E}_{2,i,r}(X) \leq O(\gamma)$  and  $\mathcal{E}_{4,i,r}(X) \leq \widetilde{O}(\sigma_0)^{q-1}\mathbb{1}_{v_j,\ell \in \mathcal{V}(X)}$ , so

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | + \eta \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(O (\gamma) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)}\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right. \\ \left. + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)}\right) \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

Recall that for every  $(X,y)\in \mathcal{Z}_s$  and  $i\neq y$ , it satisfies  $\mathbf{logit}_i(F^{(t)},X) = O\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)\left(1 - \mathbf{logit}_y(F^{(t)},X)\right)$  (see Claim D.4). Therefore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t + 1)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | \leq | \langle w _ {i, r} ^ {(t)}, v _ {j, \ell} \rangle | + \eta \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {m}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \Big (O (\gamma) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)} \Big) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right. \\ \left. + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)}\right) \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {i} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right) \right] \\ + O \left(\frac {\eta N _ {s}}{k N}\right) _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \mathbb {E} _ {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} \left[ k \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \left(O (\gamma) + \mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)}\right) \left(1 - \mathbf {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right. \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\left. + \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \left(\mathcal {E} _ {1} + \mathcal {E} _ {3} + \widetilde {O} (\sigma_ {0}) ^ {q - 1} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)}\right) \left(1 - \operatorname {l o g i t} _ {y} \left(F ^ {(t)}, X\right)\right) \right] \tag {D.14}
$$

Next, one can naively verify

$$
\underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y = i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)} \right] \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {s}{k ^ {2}}\right) \quad \text {a n d} \quad \underset {(X, y) \sim \mathcal {Z} _ {s}} {\mathbb {E}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {y \neq i} \mathbb {1} _ {v _ {j, \ell} \in \mathcal {V} (X)} \right] \leq \widetilde {O} \left(\frac {s}{k}\right)
$$

Furthermore, by Claim D.17, we have

$$
